    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    //return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return 'counter
            return False

            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
                undefinedFunction(word)
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Actually known solution:
```
forbidden = set(forbidden)
ans=0
left=0
for right in range(len(word)):
    for k in range(1, min(10, right-left+1)+1):
        if word[right-k+1:right+1] in forbidden:
            left = max(left, right-k+2)
    ans = max(ans, right-left+1)
```
This works because if any forbidden substring ending at right starts at right-k+1, then left must be after that start. Since all forbidden lengths <=10, only check last 10 chars. Another variant with trie scanning from right to left.

Let's formalize algorithm:

- Build trie of forbidden words. Store maybe `end` boolean.
- `max_len = max(len(f) for f in forbidden)` if forbidden else 0.
- `n = len(word)`
- `j = n` (exclusive right boundary)
- `ans = 0`
- Iterate `i` from n-1 down to 0:
  - Find if there is any forbidden word starting at `i` within `word[i:j]`. Since j is exclusive and j may be > i+max_len, only need check up to `limit = min(j, i + max_len)`.
  - Traverse trie from root over `word[k]` for k from i to limit-1:
    - if char not in current node: break (no forbidden prefix starting at i)
    - move to child
    - if "end" in node: found forbidden length `length = k - i + 1`; update `j = min(j, i + length - 1)`? Wait original sets j = i + length - 1. But should we use min? As argued, length <= j-i, so i+length-1 <= j-1, so assignment is same as min. But if we limit to max_len, length <= max_len and j may be large; i+length-1 <= j-1? Since length <= j-i? We only check up to limit = min(j, i+max_len). If found at length <= limit-i <= j-i, so yes. So assignment okay. But to be safe, use `j = min(j, i + length - 1)`? If length <= j-i, min gives same. If due to bug maybe not. Use min for robustness.
    - break after first found? Need consider if a shorter forbidden and a longer forbidden both start at i. The shortest gives smallest j, which is most restrictive. Is it always okay to break at first found? Suppose forbidden "a" and "abc". At i, if "a" found, j=i. That means no non-empty substring starting at i is valid. That's correct because "a" is forbidden. If forbidden "ab" and "abc", first "ab" found length2, j=i+1. But maybe a valid substring starting at i could be length? It cannot include "ab", so max length 1. Correct. So break at first (shortest) is fine. But what if a shorter forbidden appears later in trie? Trie traversal naturally returns when end encountered. If there is a shorter forbidden, it is encountered before longer. Good.
  - After possible update, `ans = max(ans, j - i)`. Since j exclusive, length of word[i:j] = j-i. But if j > n? no. If j <= i, length 0. Good.
- Return ans.

Let's craft final code:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        n = len(word)
        j = n
        ans = 0

        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            t = trie
            limit = min(j, i + max_len)
            for k in range(i, limit):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    break
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    length = k - i + 1
                    j = min(j, i + length - 1)
                    break

            ans = max(ans, j - i)

        return ans
```

Now, let's maybe produce a more minimal fix preserving original code structure:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def first_forbidden(i, j):
            t = trie
            for k in range(i, min(j, i + max_len)):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return k - i + 1
            return False

        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            length = first_forbidden(i, j)
            if length:
                j = min(j, i + length - 1)
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```

Potential issue: If `max_len` is 0, `range(i, min(j, i))` empty. Good.

Potential issue: If `j` is updated to `i + length - 1`, and `length` is an int. Good.

Potential issue: If `first_forbidden` returns False, but `False` is 0, and `if length:` false. Good.

The standard solution with trie scans from right to left and maintains `j` as the rightmost valid boundary. Actually I recall a solution:

```
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        forbidden = set(forbidden)
        ans = 0
        left = 0
        for right in range(len(word)):
            for i in range(right, max(right-10, left-1), -1):
                if word[i:right+1] in forbidden:
                    left = i + 1
                    break
            ans = max(ans, right-left+1)
        return ans
```

Now, let's think about if the final code should be exactly:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        if any(f == "" for f in forbidden):
            return 0

        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def first_forbidden(i: int, j: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            for k in range(i, min(j, i + max_len)):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return k - i + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            length = first_forbidden(i, j)
            if length:
                j = min(j, i + length - 1)
            ans = max(ans, j - i)
        return ans
```

This is clean. But note `first_forbidden` returns 0 for no forbidden, and `if length:` works. It returns int. Good.

Potential issue: If a forbidden word has length 0, we return0 before building. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
in final with code.</think>```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        if any(f == "" for f in forbidden):
            return 0

        trie = {}
        max_len = 0

        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def first_forbidden(i: int, j: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            for k in range(i, min(j, i + max_len)):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return k - i + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        j = len(word)

        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            length = first_forbidden(i, j)
            if length:
                j = min(j, i + length - 1)
            ans = max(ans, j - i)

        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        if any(f == "" for f in forbidden):
            return 0

        trie = {}
        max_len = 0

        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def first_forbidden(i: int, j: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            for k in range(i, min(j, i + max_len)):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return k - i + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        j = len(word)

        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            length = first_forbidden(i, j)
            if length:
                j = min(j, i + length - 1)
            ans = max(ans, j - i)

        return ans
